SELECT items.itemID, items.key, itemTypes.typeName, items.dateAdded
FROM items
JOIN itemTypes ON itemTypes.itemTypeID = items.itemTypeID
LEFT JOIN itemAttachments ON itemAttachments.itemID = items.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = items.itemID
WHERE items.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
  AND itemTypes.typeName NOT IN ('annotation', 'note')
  AND (
      itemTypes.typeName != 'attachment'
      OR (
          itemAttachments.parentItemID IS NULL
          AND itemAttachments.contentType = 'application/pdf'
      )
ORDER BY items.itemID
Unknown SQLite error
SELECT itemData.itemID, fields.fieldName, itemDataValues.value
FROM itemData
JOIN fields ON fields.fieldID = itemData.fieldID
JOIN itemDataValues ON itemDataValues.valueID = itemData.valueID
JOIN items ON items.itemID = itemData.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = items.itemID
WHERE items.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
ORDER BY itemData.itemID, itemData.fieldID
SELECT itemCreators.itemID,
       creators.firstName,
       creators.lastName,
       creators.fieldMode,
       itemCreators.orderIndex,
       creatorTypes.creatorType
FROM itemCreators
JOIN creators ON creators.creatorID = itemCreators.creatorID
JOIN creatorTypes ON creatorTypes.creatorTypeID = itemCreators.creatorTypeID
JOIN items ON items.itemID = itemCreators.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = items.itemID
WHERE items.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
  AND creatorTypes.creatorType IN ('author', 'editor')
ORDER BY itemCreators.itemID, itemCreators.orderIndex
SELECT collectionItems.itemID, collectionItems.collectionID
FROM collectionItems
JOIN items ON items.itemID = collectionItems.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = items.itemID
WHERE items.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
ORDER BY collectionItems.itemID, collectionItems.collectionID
SELECT itemAttachments.itemID,
       itemAttachments.parentItemID,
       itemAttachments.linkMode,
       itemAttachments.path,
       attachmentItems.key,
       attachmentItems.dateAdded
FROM itemAttachments
JOIN items AS attachmentItems ON attachmentItems.itemID = itemAttachments.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = attachmentItems.itemID
WHERE attachmentItems.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
  AND itemAttachments.contentType = 'application/pdf'
ORDER BY COALESCE(itemAttachments.parentItemID, itemAttachments.itemID), itemAttachments.itemID
SELECT itemNotes.parentItemID, itemNotes.note
FROM itemNotes
JOIN items AS noteItems ON noteItems.itemID = itemNotes.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = noteItems.itemID
WHERE noteItems.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
  AND itemNotes.parentItemID IS NOT NULL
ORDER BY itemNotes.parentItemID, itemNotes.itemID
SELECT itemAnnotations.parentItemID,
       itemAnnotations.type,
       itemAnnotations.text,
       itemAnnotations.comment,
       itemAnnotations.color,
       itemAnnotations.pageLabel,
       itemAnnotations.sortIndex,
       itemAnnotations.position
FROM itemAnnotations
JOIN items AS annotationItems ON annotationItems.itemID = itemAnnotations.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = annotationItems.itemID
WHERE annotationItems.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
ORDER BY itemAnnotations.parentItemID, itemAnnotations.sortIndex, itemAnnotations.itemID

Lattice/HelpBackupPage.swift
Backup & Restore
Lattice stores your library (metadata, annotations, tags, collections) in a local database. The Backup feature allows you to export this entire database as an archive (.latticebackup) for safekeeping, migrating to another Mac, or recovering from a previous state.
Creating a Backup
Restoring from Backup
Zotero Migration
The Backup settings tab also provides a tool to import your existing library from Zotero. Select your Zotero data directory to quickly migrate your references.
Go to Settings 
 Backup. Click 'Restore' and select a previously saved .latticebackup file.
Restoring an archive will entirely replace your current library data with the contents of the backup.
If your backup included linked PDFs, Lattice will ask you to choose a folder to extract them into. Otherwise, Lattice will automatically reconnect to your PDFs as long as they remain in their original folders.
Go to Settings 
 Backup. Click 'Backup' and choose a location to save the archive.
If you enable 'Include linked PDFs', Lattice will bundle copies of your actual PDF files into the archive. This ensures your PDFs are safely backed up together with your data.
If 'Include linked PDFs' is disabled, the backup will only contain your library data. Be sure to back up your PDF folders separately.
Lattice/HelpCitationPage.swift
Export Field Toggles
In Settings 
 Citation, control which fields are included when exporting BibTeX or RIS.
Each field has its own checkbox: Title, Author, Year, Journal, DOI, Volume, Number (Issue), Pages, Abstract
These toggles affect BibTeX and RIS export only; they do not affect paper metadata.
Configure the citation key generation format in Settings 
 Citation. The format template supports a live preview.
First author's last name (lowercase)
First author's last name (capitalized)
Full publication year
First meaningful word of the title
Lattice maintains a canonical citekey on each paper for display, copy, search, and export
Changing the template or relevant title, author, or year metadata updates those maintained citekeys

 einstein:1905
Lattice/HelpExportPage.swift
Via the toolbar export button, select 'Export Detailed...', then choose an output folder. Each paper is exported as a separate file.
Without annotation images: .md file; with annotation images: .textbundle
Content is rendered by the metadata template and annotation template together
Filename uses the maintained citekey
Duplicate filenames are auto-suffixed with numbers to avoid overwriting
Export failures show specific error messages
Template settings
Via the toolbar export button or 
E, select 'Export BibTeX...' to save references as a .bib file. You can export selected papers, the current filtered results, or the entire library.
Citekeys are automatically generated per the format in Settings; duplicates are suffixed with letters (a, b, c...)
Settings 
 Citation updates the maintained citekeys used for display, copy, search, and export
Export fields are controlled by checkboxes in Settings 
 Citation and are shared by BibTeX and RIS export
If you just want to quickly copy citation data, you don't need to export a file first:
Select one or more papers in the paper list, then right-click to copy BibTeX to clipboard.
Lattice/HelpImportPage.swift
Auto-Assignment on Import
When a collection or tag is selected in the sidebar, all newly imported papers are automatically assigned to that collection or tag.
Applies to all import methods: PDF, BibTeX, DOI
Only triggered when a collection or tag is selected; smart filters (like 'All' or 'Unfiled') do not trigger it
When replacing an existing paper, it also gains the currently selected collection or tag
To batch-import papers into a specific collection, select the target collection in the sidebar before importing.
Learn about collection and tag management
Choose File > Add via DOI... to open the DOI search panel
Enter a DOI to automatically fetch metadata from public services
Preview title, authors, journal, and year before importing
Automatic duplicate detection against existing library
File > Add via DOI...  Open DOI search panel
Add references to your Lattice library from PDF files, BibTeX files, or folders.
Click the toolbar + button and choose 'Import Files...', or press 
I to open the file picker
Select PDFs, BibTeX (.bib) files, or folders 
 Lattice detects the type automatically
Batch import: select multiple PDFs or an entire folder; folders are automatically expanded into PDF lists
You can select multiple .bib files at once 
 all entries are merged into a single import batch
Drag-and-drop: drag PDF files or folders directly into the paper list
Import panel: check or uncheck individual papers; the top shows 'X of Y selected', the bottom has Cancel (Escape) and Import (Enter) buttons
Conflict handling: when duplicates are detected, choose to skip, replace, or check 'Apply to all' for batch processing
Lattice automatically extracts local metadata signals from the PDF:
After import, Lattice automatically queries online services for complete metadata:
After import, metadata is automatically retrieved in the background. To re-fetch, select a paper and right-click 'Refresh Metadata'.
BibTeX import supports:
Supported entry types: @article, @book, @inbook, @inproceedings, @misc, etc.
Supports @string macro definitions and # concatenation syntax
Supports braces {}, quotes "", bare values, and parenthesis () delimiters
@comment and @preamble entries are automatically skipped

Uploads raw PDF bytes to an existing paper when PDF upload is available.
The current local listener address is:
All documented endpoints below are relative to this API prefix:
If you change the port in Settings, restart or reconnect any external client so it talks to the new address.
Lattice exposes an HTTP interface on the local machine so external tools can search your library, fetch citation metadata, and create papers without linking against SwiftData or the app bundle.
You can disable the listener entirely in Settings 
 Local API.
The listener uses the port configured in Settings 
 Local API.
All endpoints live under /api/v1 and return JSON.
The API is intended for local integrations such as scripts, automations, and companion tools.
Write access is off while Read-Only Mode is enabled. Turn Read-Only Mode off in Settings 
 Local API before calling write endpoints on /api/v1.
The browser extension depends on Local API being enabled, but its own write behavior is controlled by the Browser Extension toggle in Settings 
 Local API.
Open Browser Extension help
Browser Extension
The browser extension saves papers from your browser into Lattice. To use it, first enable Settings 
 Local API 
 Enabled so the extension can connect to the app.
Turn on Settings 
 Local API 
 Browser Extension to allow browser saves.
Choose a PDF folder in Settings 
 Local API if you want browser saves to attach downloaded PDFs instead of saving metadata only.
In the popup, the extension previews the current paper, lets you choose collections and tags, and then saves into your Lattice library.
If Lattice finds a duplicate paper, the popup can ask whether to skip it, replace it, or open the existing paper in Lattice.
Browser PDF uploads prefer the dedicated browser-extension folder. If you do not choose one, they fall back to the General PDF organize folder when it is configured.
If no PDF destination exists, the extension can still save paper metadata, but it will not attach the PDF file.
If you later change the Local API port, the extension reconnects automatically.
Safari support ships inside the main Lattice app. Before using it, enable the extension in Safari Settings 
 Extensions.

Lattice/HelpShortcutsPage.swift
Quickly run Export Paper Metadata via Spotlight and paste results into a note-taking app
Automate reference management workflows
Combine freely with other Shortcuts actions to build custom workflows
When using Copy to Clipboard mode, a success notification is shown after completion.
Available in the macOS Shortcuts app and Siri. Can also be quickly invoked via Spotlight search.
In Spotlight, type 'Export Paper Metadata', select a paper when prompted, and it runs immediately. The default output mode is 'Copy to Clipboard', which copies the rendered metadata directly for easy pasting into note-taking apps.
Paper: select one or more papers from your library
Output mode: return text / copy to clipboard
Include annotations: choose whether to include PDF annotations in output
Output is rendered using the metadata template configured in Settings; annotation images use text placeholder mode.
You can bind this shortcut to a keyboard shortcut in the macOS Shortcuts app.
Lattice/HelpTemplatePage.swift
Metadata Template Variables
Annotation Template Variables
Metadata Template Example
Annotation Template Example
A typical annotation template looks like this:
- **[{{type}}]** (p. {{page}})
  {{text}}
  {{#comment}}> {{comment}}{{/comment}}
A typical metadata template looks like this:
# {{title}}
**Authors:** {{authors}}
{{#year}}**Year:** {{year}}{{/year}}
{{#journal}}**Journal:** {{journal}}{{/journal}}
{{#doi}}**DOI:** {{doi}}{{/doi}}
{{#abstract}}
## Abstract
{{abstract}}
{{/abstract}}
{{#notes}}
## Notes
{{notes}}
{{/notes}}
{{#annotations}}
## Annotations
{{annotations}}
{{/annotations}}

Selected / annotated text
the speed of light...
Annotation color
Shape annotation image
(image reference)

Journal / source
Annalen der Physik
10.1002/andp.19053221004
978-0-123456-78-9
Flat collection names the paper belongs to
{{collectionPaths}}
Full hierarchical collection paths (token-safe)
#AI #ML/Transformer
Important, To Read
Lattice internal link
lattice://paper/550E8400-...
Attached PDF file URL
file:///Users/.../paper.pdf
Annotation content (rendered by the annotation template)
For hierarchical collection paths, use {{collectionPaths}} (plural). {{collectionPath}} is not a valid variable.
Variable substitution 
 outputs the field value; empty fields output an empty string
{{#field}}...{{/field}}
Conditional block 
 only rendered when the field is non-empty; can nest {{variable}} inside
Example: when an abstract exists, output the Abstract heading and content; otherwise the entire block is omitted:
{{#abstract}}
## Abstract
{{abstract}}
{{/abstract}}
Templates are used for 'Detailed Export' and the Export Paper Metadata shortcut
Two independent templates: metadata template (overall output format) and annotation template (per-annotation rendering)

 Template

SELECT items.itemID, items.key, itemTypes.typeName, items.dateAdded
FROM items
JOIN itemTypes ON itemTypes.itemTypeID = items.itemTypeID
LEFT JOIN itemAttachments ON itemAttachments.itemID = items.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = items.itemID
WHERE items.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
  AND itemTypes.typeName NOT IN ('annotation', 'note')
  AND (
      itemTypes.typeName != 'attachment'
      OR (
          itemAttachments.parentItemID IS NULL
          AND itemAttachments.contentType = 'application/pdf'
      )
ORDER BY items.itemID
Unknown SQLite error
SELECT itemData.itemID, fields.fieldName, itemDataValues.value
FROM itemData
JOIN fields ON fields.fieldID = itemData.fieldID
JOIN itemDataValues ON itemDataValues.valueID = itemData.valueID
JOIN items ON items.itemID = itemData.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = items.itemID
WHERE items.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
ORDER BY itemData.itemID, itemData.fieldID
SELECT itemCreators.itemID,
       creators.firstName,
       creators.lastName,
       creators.fieldMode,
       itemCreators.orderIndex,
       creatorTypes.creatorType
FROM itemCreators
JOIN creators ON creators.creatorID = itemCreators.creatorID
JOIN creatorTypes ON creatorTypes.creatorTypeID = itemCreators.creatorTypeID
JOIN items ON items.itemID = itemCreators.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = items.itemID
WHERE items.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
  AND creatorTypes.creatorType IN ('author', 'editor')
ORDER BY itemCreators.itemID, itemCreators.orderIndex
SELECT collectionItems.itemID, collectionItems.collectionID
FROM collectionItems
JOIN items ON items.itemID = collectionItems.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = items.itemID
WHERE items.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
ORDER BY collectionItems.itemID, collectionItems.collectionID
SELECT itemAttachments.itemID,
       itemAttachments.parentItemID,
       itemAttachments.linkMode,
       itemAttachments.path,
       attachmentItems.key,
       attachmentItems.dateAdded
FROM itemAttachments
JOIN items AS attachmentItems ON attachmentItems.itemID = itemAttachments.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = attachmentItems.itemID
WHERE attachmentItems.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
  AND itemAttachments.contentType = 'application/pdf'
ORDER BY COALESCE(itemAttachments.parentItemID, itemAttachments.itemID), itemAttachments.itemID
SELECT itemNotes.parentItemID, itemNotes.note
FROM itemNotes
JOIN items AS noteItems ON noteItems.itemID = itemNotes.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = noteItems.itemID
WHERE noteItems.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
  AND itemNotes.parentItemID IS NOT NULL
ORDER BY itemNotes.parentItemID, itemNotes.itemID
SELECT itemAnnotations.parentItemID,
       itemAnnotations.type,
       itemAnnotations.text,
       itemAnnotations.comment,
       itemAnnotations.color,
       itemAnnotations.pageLabel,
       itemAnnotations.sortIndex,
       itemAnnotations.position
FROM itemAnnotations
JOIN items AS annotationItems ON annotationItems.itemID = itemAnnotations.itemID
LEFT JOIN deletedItems ON deletedItems.itemID = annotationItems.itemID
WHERE annotationItems.libraryID = 1
  AND deletedItems.itemID IS NULL
ORDER BY itemAnnotations.parentItemID, itemAnnotations.sortIndex, itemAnnotations.itemID

Lattice/HelpBackupPage.swift
Backup & Restore
Lattice stores your library (metadata, annotations, tags, collections) in a local database. The Backup feature allows you to export this entire database as an archive (.latticebackup) for safekeeping, migrating to another Mac, or recovering from a previous state.
Creating a Backup
Restoring from Backup
Zotero Migration
The Backup settings tab also provides a tool to import your existing library from Zotero. Select your Zotero data directory to quickly migrate your references.
Go to Settings 
 Backup. Click 'Restore' and select a previously saved .latticebackup file.
Restoring an archive will entirely replace your current library data with the contents of the backup.
If your backup included linked PDFs, Lattice will ask you to choose a folder to extract them into. Otherwise, Lattice will automatically reconnect to your PDFs as long as they remain in their original folders.
Go to Settings 
 Backup. Click 'Backup' and choose a location to save the archive.
If you enable 'Include linked PDFs', Lattice will bundle copies of your actual PDF files into the archive. This ensures your PDFs are safely backed up together with your data.
If 'Include linked PDFs' is disabled, the backup will only contain your library data. Be sure to back up your PDF folders separately.
Lattice/HelpCitationPage.swift
Export Field Toggles
In Settings 
 Citation, control which fields are included when exporting BibTeX or RIS.
Each field has its own checkbox: Title, Author, Year, Journal, DOI, Volume, Number (Issue), Pages, Abstract
These toggles affect BibTeX and RIS export only; they do not affect paper metadata.
Configure the citation key generation format in Settings 
 Citation. The format template supports a live preview.
First author's last name (lowercase)
First author's last name (capitalized)
Full publication year
First meaningful word of the title
Lattice maintains a canonical citekey on each paper for display, copy, search, and export
Changing the template or relevant title, author, or year metadata updates those maintained citekeys
{auth}:{year} 
 einstein:1905
Lattice/HelpExportPage.swift
Via the toolbar export button, select 'Export Detailed...', then choose an output folder. Each paper is exported as a separate file.
Without annotation images: .md file; with annotation images: .textbundle
Content is rendered by the metadata template and annotation template together
Filename uses the maintained citekey
Duplicate filenames are auto-suffixed with numbers to avoid overwriting
Export failures show specific error messages
Template settings
Via the toolbar export button or 
E, select 'Export BibTeX...' to save references as a .bib file. You can export selected papers, the current filtered results, or the entire library.
Citekeys are automatically generated per the format in Settings; duplicates are suffixed with letters (a, b, c...)
Settings 
 Citation updates the maintained citekeys used for display, copy, search, and export
Export fields are controlled by checkboxes in Settings 
 Citation and are shared by BibTeX and RIS export
If you just want to quickly copy citation data, you don't need to export a file first:
Select one or more papers in the paper list, then right-click to copy BibTeX to clipboard.
Lattice/HelpImportPage.swift
Auto-Assignment on Import
When a collection or tag is selected in the sidebar, all newly imported papers are automatically assigned to that collection or tag.
Applies to all import methods: PDF, BibTeX, DOI
Only triggered when a collection or tag is selected; smart filters (like 'All' or 'Unfiled') do not trigger it
When replacing an existing paper, it also gains the currently selected collection or tag
To batch-import papers into a specific collection, select the target collection in the sidebar before importing.
Learn about collection and tag management
Choose File > Add via DOI... to open the DOI search panel
Enter a DOI to automatically fetch metadata from public services
Preview title, authors, journal, and year before importing
Automatic duplicate detection against existing library
File > Add via DOI...  Open DOI search panel
Add references to your Lattice library from PDF files, BibTeX files, or folders.
Click the toolbar + button and choose 'Import Files...', or press 
I to open the file picker
Select PDFs, BibTeX (.bib) files, or folders 
 Lattice detects the type automatically
Batch import: select multiple PDFs or an entire folder; folders are automatically expanded into PDF lists
You can select multiple .bib files at once 
 all entries are merged into a single import batch
Drag-and-drop: drag PDF files or folders directly into the paper list
Import panel: check or uncheck individual papers; the top shows 'X of Y selected', the bottom has Cancel (Escape) and Import (Enter) buttons
Conflict handling: when duplicates are detected, choose to skip, replace, or check 'Apply to all' for batch processing
Lattice automatically extracts local metadata signals from the PDF:
After import, Lattice automatically queries online services for complete metadata:
After import, metadata is automatically retrieved in the background. To re-fetch, select a paper and right-click 'Refresh Metadata'.
BibTeX import supports:
Supported entry types: @article, @book, @inbook, @inproceedings, @misc, etc.
Supports @string macro definitions and # concatenation syntax
Supports braces {}, quotes "", bare values, and parenthesis () delimiters
@comment and @preamble entries are automatically skipped

Returns a full citation payload for one paper, including CSL-ready metadata.
Returns assignable collections for a write client.
Returns assignable tags for a write client.
Creates one paper from a JSON request body when Read-Only Mode is off.
/papers/<uuid>/pdf
Uploads raw PDF bytes to an existing paper when PDF upload is available.
The current local listener address is:
All documented endpoints below are relative to this API prefix:
If you change the port in Settings, restart or reconnect any external client so it talks to the new address.
Lattice exposes an HTTP interface on the local machine so external tools can search your library, fetch citation metadata, and create papers without linking against SwiftData or the app bundle.
You can disable the listener entirely in Settings 
 Local API.
The listener uses the port configured in Settings 
 Local API.
All endpoints live under /api/v1 and return JSON.
The API is intended for local integrations such as scripts, automations, and companion tools.
Write access is off while Read-Only Mode is enabled. Turn Read-Only Mode off in Settings 
 Local API before calling write endpoints on /api/v1.
The browser extension depends on Local API being enabled, but its own write behavior is controlled by the Browser Extension toggle in Settings 
 Local API.
Open Browser Extension help
Browser Extension
The browser extension saves papers from your browser into Lattice. To use it, first enable Settings 
 Local API 
 Enabled so the extension can connect to the app.
Turn on Settings 
 Local API 
 Browser Extension to allow browser saves.
Choose a PDF folder in Settings 
 Local API if you want browser saves to attach downloaded PDFs instead of saving metadata only.
In the popup, the extension previews the current paper, lets you choose collections and tags, and then saves into your Lattice library.
If Lattice finds a duplicate paper, the popup can ask whether to skip it, replace it, or open the existing paper in Lattice.
Browser PDF uploads prefer the dedicated browser-extension folder. If you do not choose one, they fall back to the General PDF organize folder when it is configured.
If no PDF destination exists, the extension can still save paper metadata, but it will not attach the PDF file.
If you later change the Local API port, the extension reconnects automatically.
Safari support ships inside the main Lattice app. Before using it, enable the extension in Safari Settings 
 Extensions.

Lattice/HelpQuickStartPage.swift
Welcome to Lattice
Lattice is a native macOS reference manager 
 lightweight and elegant.
Three-Column Layout
Step 1: Import References
Step 2: Browse and Organize
Step 3: Export Citations
When you first get started, try dragging a PDF into the window 
 Lattice will automatically extract the DOI and retrieve complete reference information from multiple academic databases.
When you need to cite references in your paper, export as BibTeX or use templates to generate detailed reference notes.
Learn more about exporting
Use collections, tags, and ratings to organize your library:
Create collections to group related references by project or topic
Add tags to references with custom colors
Use star ratings to mark important papers
Lattice supports multiple import methods to quickly build your library:
Click + in the toolbar and choose 'Import Files...' to select PDFs, BibTeX files, or folders
Drag PDF files or folders into the paper list
Add a paper by DOI
After import, Lattice automatically retrieves and fills in metadata.
Learn more about importing
You can quickly migrate your existing references from Zotero in Settings 
 Backup.
Lattice uses a three-column layout for efficient reference management:
Sidebar 
 collections and tags for organizing your library
Paper list 
 a sortable table showing title, authors, year, rating, and source
Detail panel 
 view and edit metadata or annotations
Lattice/HelpShortcutsPage.swift
Quickly run Export Paper Metadata via Spotlight and paste results into a note-taking app
Automate reference management workflows
Combine freely with other Shortcuts actions to build custom workflows
When using Copy to Clipboard mode, a success notification is shown after completion.
Available in the macOS Shortcuts app and Siri. Can also be quickly invoked via Spotlight search.
In Spotlight, type 'Export Paper Metadata', select a paper when prompted, and it runs immediately. The default output mode is 'Copy to Clipboard', which copies the rendered metadata directly for easy pasting into note-taking apps.
Paper: select one or more papers from your library
Output mode: return text / copy to clipboard
Include annotations: choose whether to include PDF annotations in output
Output is rendered using the metadata template configured in Settings; annotation images use text placeholder mode.
You can bind this shortcut to a keyboard shortcut in the macOS Shortcuts app.
Lattice/HelpTemplatePage.swift
Metadata Template Variables
Annotation Template Variables
Metadata Template Example
Annotation Template Example
A typical annotation template looks like this:
- **[{{type}}]** (p. {{page}})
  {{text}}
  {{#comment}}> {{comment}}{{/comment}}
A typical metadata template looks like this:
# {{title}}
**Authors:** {{authors}}
{{#year}}**Year:** {{year}}{{/year}}
{{#journal}}**Journal:** {{journal}}{{/journal}}
{{#doi}}**DOI:** {{doi}}{{/doi}}
{{#abstract}}
## Abstract
{{abstract}}
{{/abstract}}
{{#notes}}
## Notes
{{notes}}
{{/notes}}
{{#annotations}}
## Annotations
{{annotations}}
{{/annotations}}
The following variables are available in the annotation template, rendered independently for each annotation:
Selected / annotated text
the speed of light...
Annotation color
Shape annotation image
(image reference)
The following variables are available in the metadata template:
Journal / source
Annalen der Physik
10.1002/andp.19053221004
978-0-123456-78-9
Flat collection names the paper belongs to
{{collectionPaths}}
Full hierarchical collection paths (token-safe)
#AI #ML/Transformer
Important, To Read
Lattice internal link
lattice://paper/550E8400-...
Attached PDF file URL
file:///Users/.../paper.pdf
Annotation content (rendered by the annotation template)
For hierarchical collection paths, use {{collectionPaths}} (plural). {{collectionPath}} is not a valid variable.
Variable substitution 
 outputs the field value; empty fields output an empty string
{{#field}}...{{/field}}
Conditional block 
 only rendered when the field is non-empty; can nest {{variable}} inside
Example: when an abstract exists, output the Abstract heading and content; otherwise the entire block is omitted:
{{#abstract}}
## Abstract
{{abstract}}
{{/abstract}}
Templates are used for 'Detailed Export' and the Export Paper Metadata shortcut
Two independent templates: metadata template (overall output format) and annotation template (per-annotation rendering)
Edit in Settings 
 Template
Each template has a 'Restore' button to reset to the built-in default (only available after modification)
